### [1] [Predictive Holonomy and Minimal Completion of Coarse Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13951)
*Maxim V. Churilov*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文研究粗粒化动力系统的路径依赖问题，证明了这种依赖性源于缺失状态，并提出通过Ho-Kalman分解构造最小自治补全的方法，同时给出了隐藏预测坐标的有限样本奇异值证书和最小反事实预测状态的判别准则。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 粗粒化描述的动力系统无法自主演化：先演化再粗粒化与先粗粒化再演化会给出不同预测，压缩传播子不满足半群律。这种路径依赖性表明存在缺失的状态信息。现有理论缺乏将粗粒化转化为可构造的状态补全问题的系统方法，也未能量化隐藏坐标的统计可检测性。

Method: 针对有限连续时间马尔可夫链，引入粗粒化通道C、随机截面R以及投影算子P=RC和Q=I-P。推导精确粗粒化传播子满足的带记忆核Volterra积分方程M(t)=CLQ·e^(tQLQ)·QLR。利用短时导数递归恢复所有记忆射流M^(k)(0)，通过分块Hankel算子H_∞的秩确定最小辅助状态变量数目。采用Ho-Kalman分解构造唯一（相似意义下）的最小自治补全。分析光滑f-散度的时间标度行为t^(2d+4)（d为首个非零记忆射流阶数）。推导矩阵Bernstein界等有限样本奇异值证书，并基于可容许干预下的未来律等价性识别历史状态，构造最小反事实预测状态。

Result: 理论结果：记忆核的所有射流可从粗传播子短时导数递归重构；Hankel算子秩精确给出最小补全维度；光滑f-散度通用地按t^(2d+4)标度。构造性结果：通过Ho-Kalman分解获得相似唯一的最小自治补全。实证验证：马尔可夫实例实现指数4、6、8和闭合秩1、2、3。统计工具：给出包含内禀方差控制的矩阵Bernstein界等有限样本证书，用于检测隐藏预测坐标。概念澄清：区分了普通线性实现与正/马尔可夫实现的难度差异。

Conclusion: 本文将粗粒化的路径依赖问题转化为可构造的状态补全问题，建立了从观测到的非马尔可夫动力学恢复最小自治扩展的完整理论框架。Hankel算子的秩提供了缺失状态维度的精确代数刻画，Ho-Kalman分解给出了唯一最小补全的构造方法。f-散度的时间标度律和有限样本奇异值证书为隐藏状态的统计检测提供了实用工具。通过可容许干预下的未来律等价性定义的最小反事实预测状态，为因果推断提供了理论基础。该工作明确区分了线性实现的代数问题与保持正性/马尔可夫性的更难问题，为非平衡统计物理、数据驱动建模和因果推断等领域提供了新的理论工具。

Abstract: A coarse description of a dynamical system generally fails to evolve autonomously: evolving and coarse-graining in different orders gives different predictions, and the compressed propagator fails the semigroup law. We show that this path dependence is the observable boundary of missing state. For a finite continuous-time Markov chain with generator $L$, coarse channel $C$, and stochastic section $R$ satisfying $CR=I$, define $P=RC$, $Q=I-P$, and $K=CLR$. The exact coarse propagator $T_t=C\mathrm{e}^{tL}R$ obeys a Volterra equation with memory kernel $\mathcal{M}(t)=CLQ\,\mathrm{e}^{tQLQ}\,QLR$. Every memory jet $\mathcal{M}^{(k)}(0)$ is recursively recoverable from the short-time derivatives of $T_t$ alone. The rank of the block Hankel operator $\mathbb{H}_{\infty}=(\mathcal{M}^{(i+j)}(0))_{i,j\geq 0}$ is exactly the smallest number of auxiliary linear state variables required to turn the coarse dynamics into an exact autonomous, time-homogeneous system. A minimal completion is constructible by Ho--Kalman factorization and is unique up to similarity. If $d=\min\{k:\mathcal{M}^{(k)}(0)\neq 0\}$, then every smooth classical $f$-divergence generically scales as $t^{2d+4}$. Exact Markov examples realize exponents $4,6,8$ and closure ranks $1,2,3$. We also derive finite-sample singular-value certificates for hidden predictive coordinates, including a matrix-Bernstein bound with intrinsic-variance control. Finally, histories are identified when they induce the same future law under every admissible intervention, yielding the minimal counterfactual predictive state. The results turn coarse-graining into a constructive state-completion problem and separate ordinary linear realization from the harder requirement of a positive or Markov realization.

</details>


### [2] [Detector-Frame Quantum Limits for Heterodyne Timing in Relativistic Optical Links](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13963)
*Salman Sajad Wani,Aatif Kaisar Khan,Saif Al-Kuwari,Mir Faizal*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文针对相对论效应下的空间光学链路异差相位跟踪问题，建立了探测器框架理论，推导了量子限制下的相位测量精度界限，并揭示了立方根标度规律用于优化开环积分窗口和残余相位误差。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 空间干涉测量和卫星量子网络中的长基线光学链路需要在多普勒运动和引力频移作用下进行异差相位跟踪。相对论频移会在有限读出窗口内产生大的探测器框架拍频偏移，从而限制前端相位读出的时间精度。现有理论缺乏针对相对论光学链路中量子限制异差定时的探测器框架基准，需要明确全局动力学界限如何影响任务受限的连续光学测量。

Method: 采用有效双模信号-本振模型建立探测器框架，其中相对论运动重新标度本振频率。针对代表性高斯资源推导闭式Mandelstam-Tamm和Margolus-Levitin界限，将全局态可区分性与异差相位跟踪的操作精度联系起来。在强本振regime下分析几何-操作失配现象。结合开环瞬态捕获分析，研究白相位噪声和近似恒定的相对论拍频漂移对系统性能的影响。

Result: 在强本振条件下发现几何-操作失配：联合光学态可以在任意短的全局时间尺度上演化，而相关信息速率由测量的探测器记录决定。对于白相位噪声和在捕获窗口内近似恒定的相对论拍频漂移，最优预锁定开环积分窗口和最小残余相位误差呈现立方根标度规律。建立了相对论光学链路中量子限制异差定时的探测器框架基准。

Conclusion: 本研究为相对论光学链路中的量子限制异差定时建立了探测器框架基准，阐明了全局动力学界限如何进入任务受限的连续光学测量。立方根标度规律为优化开环积分窗口和最小化残余相位误差提供了理论指导，对空间干涉测量和卫星量子网络的实际应用具有重要意义。该理论框架连接了量子信息论中的基本界限与相对论效应下光学相位测量的操作精度。

Abstract: Long-baseline optical links for space interferometry and satellite quantum networks rely on heterodyne phase tracking under Doppler motion and gravitational frequency shifts. These relativistic shifts can generate large detector-frame beat-frequency offsets over finite readout windows and thereby constrain the temporal precision of front-end phase readout. We develop a detector-frame framework for this problem using an effective two-mode signal--local-oscillator model in which relativistic motion rescales the local-oscillator frequency. Within this setting we derive closed-form Mandelstam--Tamm and Margolus--Levitin bounds for representative Gaussian resources and connect global state distinguishability with the operational precision of heterodyne phase tracking. In the bright-local-oscillator regime we identify a geometric--operational mismatch: the joint optical state can evolve on arbitrarily short global timescales while the relevant information rate is set by the measured detector record. Combining this result with an open-loop transient-acquisition analysis, we show that white phase noise and relativistic beat-frequency drift that is approximately constant over the acquisition window generate a cube-root scaling benchmark for the optimal pre-lock open-loop integration window and the minimum residual phase error. These results establish detector-frame benchmarks for quantum-limited heterodyne timing in relativistic optical links and clarify how global dynamical bounds enter task-restricted continuous optical measurements.

</details>

### [4] [Empirical Scaling Laws of Quantum Vision Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13970)
*Yuqing Li,Fengyi Gao,Xin Jin,Xiaowei Jia,Qi Cheng,Junyu Liu*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本研究探讨了量子视觉变换器(QViTs)的经验缩放规律,发现验证损失遵循量子比特数、变换器深度和训练数据集大小的联合幂律模型,揭示了增加量子比特数可以在相当性能下补偿较少经典编码器参数的机制,为量子机器学习中的资源节省提供了新方向。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 量子机器学习(QML)何时以及如何实现量子优势仍不清楚,即使是多项式级别的资源改进也具有重要意义。研究者希望通过将量子计算与现代大语言模型底层的变换器架构相结合,探索量子视觉变换器是否表现出类似经典模型的缩放行为,以及当量子比特数与数据集大小和模型参数一起考虑时这种行为如何变化,从而为QML中可能的资源节省探索方向。

Method: 研究采用数值图像分类实验,系统性地研究量子视觉变换器(QViTs)在不同量子比特数、变换器深度和训练数据集大小条件下的性能表现。通过对验证损失进行建模,使用联合移位幂律模型(joint shifted power-law model)拟合量子比特数、变换器深度和训练数据集大小三个维度的关系,并将拟合结果表达为经典编码器参数预算的函数进行分析。

Result: 数值图像分类实验中的验证损失可以很好地用量子比特数、变换器深度和训练数据集大小的联合移位幂律模型来描述。将该拟合结果用经典编码器参数预算表示后,识别出一个特定区域:在该区域内,增加量子比特数可以在保持相当预测性能的情况下补偿较少的经典编码器参数。这一发现与先前关于量子学习模型参数效率的观察结果一致。

Conclusion: 量子视觉变换器表现出明确的缩放规律,存在量子比特数与经典参数之间的权衡机制。研究证实了量子学习模型的参数效率优势,并为探索量子机器学习中可能的资源节省指明了方向。这表明通过增加量子比特数,有可能在维持性能的同时减少对经典计算资源的需求,为实现量子优势提供了潜在路径。

Abstract: Quantum machine learning (QML) is a promising application of quantum computing, yet when and how practical learning models can achieve quantum advantage remains poorly understood. Even a polynomial improvement in the resources needed to attain a given predictive performance would be significant. In this work, we investigate empirical scaling laws of quantum vision transformers (QViTs), which connect QML to the transformer architecture underlying modern large language models. We ask whether QViTs exhibit scaling behavior analogous to classical models and how this behavior changes when qubit number is included alongside dataset size and model parameters. The validation losses in our numerical image-classification experiments are well described by a joint shifted power-law model in qubit number, transformer depth, and training dataset size. Expressing this fit in terms of the classical encoder parameter budget identifies a regime in which increasing the number of qubits can compensate for fewer classical encoder parameters at comparable predicted performance. This finding is consistent with earlier observations of parameter efficiency in quantum learning models and suggests a direction for exploring possible resource savings in QML.

</details>


### [5] [Parameter-Efficient Multilinear Readout for Quantum Reservoir Computing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13974)
*Van Tien Nguyen,Panagiotis Markopoulos*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文提出了多线性量子储备池计算(ML-QRC)方法，通过张量结构化的神经读出层替代标准的扁平化线性读出，利用模式投影实现参数压缩。在保持与传统方法竞争力的同时，相比全连接MLP减少了12倍参数量，并在低数据场景下展现出更好的稳定性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 标准量子储备池计算流程将储备池测量结果扁平化后训练线性读出层，这忽略了时间步、量子比特和观测量之间的自然张量结构。为了利用这种固有的多维结构并减少参数冗余，需要设计一种既能保留张量结构信息又能实现参数高效压缩的读出层架构。

Method: 提出多线性量子储备池计算(ML-QRC)方法，采用张量结构化的神经读出层，通过模式投影(mode-wise projections)进行压缩。该方法将特征到隐藏层的参数数量从64TnF降低至d(T+n+F)+64d³，其中T为时间步数，n为量子比特数，F为观测量数量，d为压缩维度。在T=50、n=6、d=8、F=n+C(n,2)的配置下，实现了12.1倍的特征块压缩率。

Result: 在Mackey-Glass预测任务上，ML-QRC与Ridge回归在统计上保持竞争力，并在低数据场景下相比神经MLP读出层展现出更好的稳定性。在TFIM(横场伊辛模型)相分类任务上，ML-QRC在完整数据集上获得了测试方法中数值最高的准确率，同时参数量比完整密集MLP少12.0倍。然而，在50%数据量的TFIM任务中，Ridge方法表现最佳，ML-QRC的低数据优势未能迁移到该任务。

Conclusion: ML-QRC最适合被视为一种保留量子储备池张量结构的压缩型神经读出层，而非在测试场景下简单线性/Ridge读出层的替代品。它在密集MLP读出层与简单线性方法之间提供了一种中间选择：通过张量分解实现显著的参数压缩，在某些任务(如低数据Mackey-Glass)上提供稳定性优势，但并不普遍优于简单的线性方法。该方法的价值在于为量子储备池计算提供了一种结构化的、参数高效的神经读出方案。

Abstract: Quantum Reservoir Computing (QRC) uses fixed quantum dynamics as a high-dimensional feature map. Still, standard pipelines flatten reservoir measurements and train a linear readout, ignoring the natural tensor structure across time steps, qubits, and observables. We propose a multilinear readout for quantum reservoir computing (ML-QRC), a tensor-structured neural readout compressed through mode-wise projections. Relative to a dense hidden-width-64 neural readout, this reduces the feature-to-hidden parameter count from $64TnF$ to $d(T+n+F)+64d^3$, giving a $12.1\times$ feature-block compression at $T=50$, $n=6$, $d=8$, and $F=n+\binom{n}{2}$. Across Mackey-Glass forecasting and TFIM phase classification, ML-QRC remains statistically competitive with Ridge on Mackey-Glass and obtains the numerically highest full-data TFIM accuracy among tested methods, while using $12.0\times$ fewer parameters than the full dense MLP. Its clearest benefit is improved low-data stability relative to neural MLP readouts on Mackey-Glass; this advantage does not transfer to TFIM, where Ridge performs best at 50\% data. Overall, ML-QRC is best viewed as a tensor-structured neural readout that compresses dense MLP readouts while preserving QRC tensor structure, rather than as a replacement for simple linear/Ridge readouts in the tested regime.

</details>


### [6] [On the Fast Electrons-Optical Modes Interaction Problem Error](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13976)
*Svetlana Churochkina,Dmitry Churochkin*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本文基于量子信息方法，通过计算保真度严格研究了相对论量子光学中自由电子Dirac旋量近似的精确性，发现保真度不仅依赖于波矢，还与波矢和自旋取向的相互关系有关，揭示了一种类手征效应，并确定了最优几何构型。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 相对论量子光学方法中使用了Ψ_k ≈ Ψ_k₀的近似替换（当|k-k₀| ≪ |k₀|时），但该近似误差的严格评估尚未完成。需要从量子信息角度精确量化这种近似的有效性，并确定其适用条件和约束。

Method: 采用量子信息论中的保真度(fidelity)作为量化工具，计算非相互作用自由电子Dirac旋量Ψ_k和Ψ_k₀之间的符合程度。通过解析推导获得保真度的显式表达式，分析其对波矢k、k₀以及自旋取向的依赖关系。

Result: 获得了保真度的解析表达式，证明保真度不可避免地依赖于：(1)波矢k和k₀；(2)[k₀,k]与⟨s⟩†σ⟨s⟩的相互取向关系（其中s为二分量自旋单位矢量，σ为泡利矩阵矢量）。这一结果表明保真度中存在类手征效应(chirality-like effect)，对将相对论量子光学方法应用于相关问题时的自旋任意性施加了约束。确定了k₀、k-k₀和⟨s⟩†σ⟨s⟩的最优几何构型。

Conclusion: 相对论量子光学中的Dirac旋量近似Ψ_k ≈ Ψ_k₀的有效性受到波矢和自旋取向的共同制约，存在类手征效应。该研究为聚焦电子束与光学模式相互作用问题中应用该近似方法提供了严格的理论基础和自旋构型优化准则，明确了该近似方法的适用边界条件。

Abstract: A recently developed relativistic quantum optics approach for a fast electron beam-optical modes interaction problem (Optica 6, 1524--1534 (2019)) contains suggestion about a smallness of the error induced by replacement $Ψ_{\vec k}\approxΨ_{\vec k_0}$ for noninteracting free electron Dirac spinors at such wave vector $\vec k$ that $\left|\vec k-\vec k_0 \right| \ll\left|\vec k_0\right|$ with $\vec k_0$ as a peaked wave vector of a focused electron beam. Here, based on quantum information approach we strictly investigated a degree of coincidence of spinors $Ψ_{\vec k}$ and $Ψ_{\vec k_0}$ by calculating fidelity for them. The analytical expression is obtained. It is shown that the fidelity inevitably depends on not only wave vectors $\vec k$ and $\vec k_0$ but also on mutual orientation of $[\vec k_0,\vec k]$ and $\hat{\vec{s}}^{\dagger}\vecσ\hat{\vec{s}}$ with $\vec{s}$ is a two-component spin unit vector and $\vecσ$ is a vector of Pauli matrices. This result can be conceived as a chirality-like effect in the fidelity and constraints spin arbitrariness when applying the developed relativistic quantum optics approach to the problems of interest. A geometrical configuration of $\vec k_0$, $\vec k-\vec k_0$, and $\hat{\vec{s}}^†\vecσ\hat{\vec{s}}$ that is optimal in terms of fidelity is established.

</details>


### [7] [Can the Quantum Fisher Effective Dimension Judge a Hybrid Quantum Neural Network?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14070)
*Md Masum Mia,Amith Khandakar,Mohamed Arselene Ayari*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: 本研究系统评估了混合量子神经网络中量子Fisher信息矩阵(QFIM)有效维度的实际含义,发现其主要表征电路几何特性而非模型质量,与测试精度的相关性不稳定且可能为负,挑战了将其作为可训练性或准确性指标的传统解读。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 有效维度(量子Fisher信息矩阵的参与比)常被解释为混合量子神经网络可训练性或准确性的指标,但这些解读很少在受控条件下得到验证。研究者希望通过系统性实验明确该指标的真实测量对象,避免对其意义的误读。

Method: 在受控实验条件下系统评估有效维度:使用2个图像数据集、4种冻结骨干网络(包含随机投影对照)、8量子比特上的4种电路深度、3个随机种子;将有效维度定义为QFIM对角线的参与比(介于1到参数总数之间);跟踪训练过程中有效维度的变化并计算其与测试精度的相关性;分析不同电路深度对参数方向激活比例的影响;测量初始化梯度方差以检测贫瘠高原现象。

Abstract: Hybrid quantum neural networks (QNNs) pair a classical feature extractor with a variational quantum circuit, and the effective dimension of the circuit's quantum Fisher information matrix (QFIM) is widely used to assess such models. A permutation-invariant Hamiltonian diagonal in the graph basis has ground-state multiplicity at least $N!/|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|$ whenever $G$ is a ground graph. The trace-distance asymmetry of an arbitrary state also bounds its probability of selecting one labeled graph.

A cyclic clock with a reversible incidence update has a unique ground state; gauge reduction yields a cycle Laplacian with a rank-one pin and the exact gap $Δ_{N,L}=E_* (1-\cosθ_L)$, where $\tan(Lθ_L/2)=1/\sinθ_L$ and $0<θ_L<π/L$. In particular, $Δ_{N,L}/E_*=π^2/(2L^2)-2π^2/L^3+O(L^{-4})$, independently of the number of vertices. In an alternative order-free architecture, one clock qubit per unordered pair gives commuting four-local terms, $2^N$ ground states and a size-independent gap $E_*(1-1/\sqrt2)$. A fixed-weight control penalty selects $\binom Nq$ ground states without reducing the gap. These exact finite results expose clock and locality resource tradeoffs; they do not establish an emergent geometric phase.

Abstract: Approximate quantum compilation (AQC) using tensor networks provides a promising route to reducing the circuit resources required for quantum simulation. However, the factors governing the compressibility of many-body dynamics and the extent to which compressed circuits retain physically relevant information on quantum hardware remain poorly understood. We investigate these questions using AQCtensor for an 80-qubit one-dimensional anisotropic XYZ Heisenberg chain initialized in a Néel state. By varying the exchange anisotropy and external magnetic field, we find that compression performance changes systematically with the anisotropic coupling configuration and field orientation. These variations are strongly associated with a nested-commutator measure related to second-order Trotter error, with corresponding directional trends also reflected in the target-state MPS bond dimension. For an 80-qubit quantum-hardware evaluation, AQCtensor compresses a 10-repetition target evolution to 5 repetitions while retaining a target-state fidelity of 0.995474, reducing the total circuit depth by 45.9% and the two-qubit gate count by 47.6%. The compressed circuit exhibits comparable behavior across magnetization, nearest-neighbor correlation, and Hamiltonian expectation measurements, while process-level diagnostics and local reduced-state tomography provide complementary characterization beyond these observables. Together, these results link AQCtensor compressibility to Hamiltonian commutator structure and target-state complexity and demonstrate substantial circuit reduction while retaining physically relevant features in an 80-qubit quantum-hardware experiment.

Abstract: A variational quantum state may have many control angles, and its complete gradient contains one derivative for each. The task is to turn access to selected derivatives into a shared finite-shot record for the complete vector. The Hopf ansatz supplies the setting: it constructs any normalized pure state from nested two-way magnitude splits and, for complex states, leaf phases, with one directly programmed angle per split or phase. Earlier Hopf constructions prepare the state, recover coordinates from known amplitudes, and provide indexed derivative access. With a phase-calibrated controlled reflection, the inverse Hopf frame maps each normalized magnitude direction to a computational label, so every all-$X$ outcome contributes to every magnitude derivative. A separate signed leaf record supplies the complex phase block. A checkpoint variant reverses only the suffix below a selected depth and returns that layer's gradient block. For a complete chart with $M$ real-valued parameters, sufficient execution counts are $\mathcal{O}(\log\log M)$ for the global method and $\mathcal{O}(\log M\log\log M)$ for the complete checkpoint schedule at fixed additive coordinate accuracy and confidence. The global saving is record reuse, not a reduction of the $M$-entry output. Under the designated direct-angle schedules, the global method's assigned Hopf CNOT work and input/output-inclusive storage have at most logarithmic overhead relative to a matched scalar evaluation. The same Hopf geometry thus carries a state forward and the objective's complete coordinate response backward.

</details>

Abstract: The codeword stabilized (CWS) framework provides a systematic method for constructing quantum error correcting codes from a simple undirected graph and a classical code. When the underlying classical code is linear, the resulting quantum code is a stabilizer code. In this work, we investigate the conditions under which an arbitrary $[n,k,d]$ classical binary linear code can be lifted to an $[[n,k,d]]$ quantum stabilizer code using the CWS framework. We establish a lower bound on the probability that the resulting quantum code preserves the minimum distance $d$ of the classical code. This probabilistic analysis yields a Gilbert-Varshamov-type condition on the $n,k,d$ parameters of the classical code that guarantees the existence of a graph resulting in a quantum code with the same $n,k,d$ parameters.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 尽管量子计算具有巨大潜力，但量子系统易受噪声和错误影响。为实现可靠的量子计算，需要开发实用的容错量子计算架构。现有研究多停留于理论或小规模演示，缺乏在真实量子硬件上部署完整容错架构并执行非平凡算法的实验验证。本研究旨在通过在先进的离子阱量子计算机上实现基于错误检测码的容错架构，并结合算法层面的错误检验机制，验证该架构在实际量子算法（行波进位加法器）中的有效性和性能提升。

Result: 1. **一比特加法器**：在应用突发错误过滤后，仅观察到1次逻辑失败，估计逻辑失败率为$(6.0 \pm 5.9) \times 10^{-5}$，远低于未编码形式的$(3.5 \pm 1.3) \times 10^{-3}$失败率；2. **二比特和三比特加法器**：同样显示出性能改进；3. **四比特和五比特加法器**：执行性能略高于阈值水平；4. **算法检验协同效应**：算法检验与错误检测码的结合有效提升了容错能力，将多个电路位置的有效容错距离从2增加到4。

Conclusion: 本研究成功在真实量子硬件上演示了基于错误检测码的通用容错量子计算架构，并通过执行行波进位加法器算法验证了其有效性。结合算法检验机制，该架构在短比特加法运算中实现了显著的逻辑错误率降低（约两个数量级），证明了错误检测码与算法层面检验的协同作用。对于较短的加法器电路（1-3比特），系统表现出明显的容错优势；而较长电路（4-5比特）的性能接近阈值，表明该方法在当前硬件条件下对中小规模量子算法具有实用价值。这为未来实现大规模容错量子计算提供了重要的实验基础和技术路径。

Abstract: We deploy a universal fault-tolerant quantum computing (FTQC) architecture based on error-detecting $[\![8,3,2]\!]$ and $[\![4,2,2]\!]$ codes on Quantinuum's Helios-1 trapped-ion quantum computer. We compile and execute ripple-carry adder (RCA) circuits for classical inputs up to five bits on this architecture. Uncomputing a workspace qubit provides an additional ``algorithmic check'' to detect errors. While available at both the physical and logical levels, this check synergizes with our architecture by increasing the effective fault distance of many circuit locations from two to four. Combining this algorithmic check with the error-detecting codes, we observed only a single logical failure in one-bit addition after burst-error filtering, yielding an estimated logical failure rate of $(6.0 \pm 5.9) \times 10^{-5}$. This is well below the $(3.5 \pm 1.3) \times 10^{-3}$ failure probability observed for one-bit addition on the same hardware in unencoded form. Two- and three-bit adders also showed performance improvements, while the longer four- and five-bit adders executed just above threshold.

Conclusion: 通过引入对数导数和Newton-Girard恒等式,本文成功简化了量子信号处理中相位因子的分解程序,消除了对Prony方法的依赖,在保持稳定性的同时提高了计算效率,为QSP的实际应用提供了更优的算法工具。

Abstract: Quantum signal processing (QSP) requires the efficient computation of phase factors associated with a polynomial transformation. One existing stable factorization method avoids direct root finding but relies on Prony's method to recover a polynomial from weighted power sums of its roots (arXiv:2202.02671). We present a modification of this approach that eliminates Prony's method by using the logarithmic derivative of an associated polynomial. Because the logarithmic derivative has the same residue at every root after accounting for multiplicity, its Fourier coefficients directly yield the unweighted power sums of the roots inside the unit disk. The Newton--Girard identities then recover the coefficients of the corresponding polynomial without computing its roots. The resulting algorithm has $O(d\log^2 d)$ computational complexity and simplifies the factorization procedure of the original method. In numerical experiments on Hamiltonian simulation, eigenstate filtering, matrix inversion, and the Fermi--Dirac operator, our implementation computes phase factors in seconds with maximum errors on the order of $10^{-12}$.

</details>

Conclusion: 复数在量子计算中不是绝对必需的，可以通过添加额外的量子比特来标记和编码复数信息。但在多方非局域性证明（如GHZ博弈）中，必须为每个参与方提供局域的标记量子比特，而不能使用单一的全局标记量子比特，以避免违反局域性条件。这一结果揭示了量子非局域性与资源之间的深层联系，表明实值形式主义在付出额外量子资源代价后可以等价地描述量子现象。

Abstract: We show that it is possible for three people to win the GHZ game with just the standard real valued misty state formalism, but each party will need to have two balls instead of one. That is, there will be six balls involved, rather than three. In fact, as a general rule, instead of using complex numbers in a quantum computer you can always add a qubit to "tag" complex numbers. But for proofs of non-locality, like the GHZ game, we cannot have a single universal tagging qubit, because all three parties would need access to it and therefore they could potentially transfer information via it. That's where we need to replace the one ball with three that each only need to be accessed locally.

Abstract: Sensing time-varying signals is essential for precision measurement and for characterizing fluctuations in physical systems. In this work, we show that quantum entanglement provides an exponential advantage in the acquisition time required to learn the temporal statistics of a stationary classical signal. The task is to estimate the characteristic function of joint displacements on selected bosonic modes within a fixed-duration window, using continuous measurements of one signal realization. We present an entanglement-assisted scheme based on two-mode squeezed probes and local Bell measurements with protected idlers, and bound its acquisition time accounting for readout noise and correlations between signal windows. For a strictly stationary pulse family, it achieves polynomial acquisition time at inverse-polynomial accuracy with logarithmic squeezing and polynomial photon number. On the other hand, we prove that any local idler-free protocols require acquisition time exponential in the number of selected temporal modes per window. This lower bound holds for both sequentially prepared and arbitrarily temporally entangled probes, allowing non-Gaussian inputs and measurements and causal classical feedback, as long as each output is measured destructively within its time bin. These results establish an exponential quantum learning advantage for a single continuously monitored stationary signal, with the sensing cost measured by total acquisition time.

By feeding the collectively generated magnetic field back to the spins below the threshold for self-sustained $J$-oscillation, we extend the effective spin coherence and cooperatively enhance the susceptibility up to $ | χ| \approx$ 80. Abstract: We present the concept of quadrature-detected zero-to-ultralow-field nuclear magnetic resonance (QD ZULF NMR). Two orthogonal sensors, atomic optically pumped magnetometers, are used to separately detect components of oscillating sample magnetization, which are then combined into a quadrature signal. This approach enables the distinction of positive and negative resonance frequencies with respect to the laboratory frame---such that absolute signs of gyromagnetic ratios can be determined. In addition, QD ZULF NMR reduces peak density and overlap compared to ZULF NMR with single-axis detection, while providing richer information about nuclear-spin dynamics. As an illustration, we present experimental $J$-spectra for thermally polarized [$^{13}$C]-formic acid and [$^{13}$C]-methanol, compounds commonly used in ZULF NMR which represent XA and XA$_3$ spin systems, respectively. A theoretical framework describing QD ZULF NMR signals is provided.

Abstract: Trapped ion quantum computing based on the quantum charge coupled device (QCCD) architecture uses functional zoning and ion transport to enable high fidelity computation and all to all qubit connectivity. This framework connects waveform design, control, and computational validation, with smooth multichannel waveforms computed rapidly through a Karush-Kuhn-Tucker(KKT) linear system. The fraction of execution time spent on transport decreases from approximately $70\%$ to $55\%$. Abstract: We investigate dynamical quantum phase transitions (DQPTs) generated by a two-step linear quench with a finite waiting time at the equilibrium critical point. The waiting stage leads to the accumulation of a dynamical phase that depends on the waiting time and directly reflects the critical spectral structure. In the transverse Ising chain, this accumulated phase modifies the critical structure of DQPTs from a single-critical-mode (SCM) regime to a multiple-critical-mode (MCM) regime. We analytically and numerically determine the boundary between the two regimes. Along this boundary, the waiting time approaches a constant in the sudden-quench limit, whereas in the slow-quench limit it follows Kibble--Zurek (KZ) scaling with the quench time. In the SCM regime, the rate function exhibits a conventional linear singularity, $r(t)-r(t^{*})\propto |t-t^{*}|^α$, with the critical exponent $α=1$ near the critical time. At the SCM--MCM boundary, this exponent changes to $α=1/2$, leading to a divergent first derivative of the rate function. Additional discrete parameter lines with $α=1/2$ also appear within the MCM regime in the parameter plane spanned by the waiting and quench times. Our findings establish a direct connection between the accumulated dynamical phase and the critical structure of DQPTs, further supported by an extended Su--Schrieffer--Heeger (SSH) model.

</details>

Abstract: When implementing scalable fault-tolerant quantum computing using superconducting quantum processors, one of the critical challenges to overcome is the presence of defective qubits arising from fabrication and calibration imperfections. A defect on a specific qubit severely restricts the connectivity between adjacent qubits, disrupts stabilizer measurements, induces frequent detection events, and consequently inflates the logical error rate. To mitigate this issue, various strategies have been proposed, such as syndrome extraction circuits based on stabilizer folding and the construction of super-stabilizers via gauge operators. However, these methods have primarily been developed for architectures with relatively high connectivity of 3 to 4. Furthermore, experimental demonstrations showing an actual reduction in logical error rates in the presence of physical hardware defects have been lacking. In this work, we propose a defect-avoidance scheme tailored for the heavy-hexagon architecture, which features a lower connectivity of 2 to 3. While previous approaches required four measurement cycles to measure all gauge operators comprising a super-stabilizer, our proposed scheme completes all gauge measurements within only two cycles. We experimentally validate this approach on IBM quantum hardware by identifying physical defects and implementing the defect-avoidance circuits. As a result, avoiding the defect yields an approximately 10\% reduction in the logical error rate per cycle for both logical $X$ and $Z$ errors. These findings demonstrate that defects in superconducting hardware can be successfully handled even under the constrained connectivity of heavy-hexagon lattices, and that defect avoidance substantially reduces logical errors on real quantum hardware.

With classical assistance, we obtain an explicit spectral threshold formula and a protocol attaining it. Classical communication strictly improves fidelity whenever $m$ is smaller than the source rank. The corresponding compression costs are two smoothed versions of the log-rank entropy, also called max-entropy: trace-distance smoothing under local operations and quadratic smoothing under LOCC. For i.i.d. copies of a fixed source with positive entropy variance, however, the two costs have identical first-, second-, and third-order asymptotic terms. For nonlattice spectra, we determine the constant-order advantage: classical communication saves $\log_2(e)-1$ bits per block asymptotically, and the ratio of assisted to unassisted minimum communication dimensions converges to $2/e$.

Abstract: Superradiance is a collective optical effect where emitters cooperatively radiate with a maximal intensity scaling proportional to the square of the number of emitters. Abstract: Quantum science and engineering increasingly target the exploitation and control of large-scale dynamical systems. As system size grows, novel phenomena and quantum advantages emerge, but the resulting many-body dynamics become correspondingly harder to monitor and characterize. In particular, the underlying Hamiltonian often lacks symmetries, and the induced many-body dynamics quickly become hard to track. Focusing on one of the most important quantum features---entanglement---we demonstrate that, for a broad class of common Hamiltonian models, one can design effective witnesses that detect both the presence and structure of entanglement over relatively long evolution times. Inspired by stabilizer-based simulation, we track the evolution of generalized stabilizers of the underlying state and use them to construct linear entanglement witnesses. Through numerical experiments on Heisenberg-type Hamiltonians with up to twelve qubits, we show that the required number of measurement settings can be kept at a modest scale as the system size increases, and entanglement detection remains effective over evolution times that significantly exceed those accessible to naive approaches. The corresponding calculations can be carried out largely symbolically. Furthermore, unlike accurate state simulation, the stabilizer-based design of entanglement witnesses can be performed in a much more coarse-grained, digitized manner. We hope these results will stimulate further exploration of the quantum control of large systems.

Abstract: Quantum generative models face growing qubit requirements and concentrated loss landscapes when learning long time series. Here we introduce holographic quantum circuit sampling (HQCS), which combines mid-circuit measurements, qubit reuse and independently parameterized temporal blocks to generate trajectories with a fixed number of qubits. For Haar-random HQCS and a normalized exponential kernel, we prove inverse-polynomial bounds on maximum mean discrepancy loss variance, contrasting with exponential concentration in global Haar-random generators. For generic members of a family of nonstationary Markov processes, temporal parameterization reduces exact-generation gate counts from quadratic to linear in sequence length relative to a specified class of repeated shared-block generators. Simulations demonstrate learning of Gaussian autoregressive processes and historical Volatility Index (VIX) trajectories, with the latter achieving over tenfold lower maximum mean discrepancy than the tested classical benchmarks. These results establish a framework for quantum time-series generation with fixed qubit requirements and provable avoidance of exponential loss concentration as sequence length grows.

Abstract: Area laws express the expectation that entanglement in gapped quantum ground states scales with a boundary rather than a volume. In one dimension this holds for every Rényi entropy, and the Rényi area-law conjecture asserts the same in higher dimensions for each fixed $0<α<1$. Here we disprove this conjecture in every spatial dimension two and higher. We construct finite-range Hamiltonians with fixed local dimension, bounded interaction strength, unique ground states and a uniform spectral gap, whose ground states exhibit volume-law Rényi entanglement while obeying a von Neumann area law and remaining connected to product states through uniformly gapped local paths. The mechanism is error-space complexity: locally identifiable components of arbitrarily small weight can encode states with high entanglement complexity. We also establish the worst-case rank-error trade-off for approximate ground-state projectors, a key tool in one-dimensional area-law proofs. At fixed gap, the optimal Schmidt rank at projection error $ε$ across a planar cut of cross-section $A$ scales as $\exp[\tildeΘ((A\logε^{-1})^{2/3})]$ when $\logε^{-1}\gtrsim A$. Thus $α=1$ is the sharp threshold for a universal Rényi area law within this adiabatically connected class.

Abstract: We perform a detailed mathematical study of the effective Hamiltonian describing a non-stationary electromagnetic cavity field, introduced by C.~K.~Law, in the presence of uniform linear motion. Although this result is mathematically consistent, the interval obtained is an order of magnitude larger than the physically admissible range $\lvert v/c \rvert < 1$. To address this limitation, we propose a reinterpretation of bosonic coupling terms as generators of the symplectic algebra $\mathrm{Sp}(4,\mathbb{R})$, identifying them with transformations that can be implemented through linear optical elements. Within this photonic approach, the coupling parameters are not restricted to small values, allowing us to explore regimes of strong effective interactions. In this case, we demonstrate the generation of two-mode squeezed states and derive analytical expressions for the average photon number in each mode. Furthermore, we analyze statistical properties that characterize mixedness and entanglement, including intermode correlations, the Mandel parameter, and the linear entropy. The corresponding Wigner function provides a phase-space visualization of the system's behavior, illustrating the connection between cavity motion, mode coupling, and nonclassical field correlations via the underlying symplectic algebra.

Abstract: Using established probabilistic measurement-reversal theory, we study recovery placement in encoded-qutrit teleportation under monitored decay. We characterize the success advantage of reversing a no-jump map only on the encoded support and compare two receiver capabilities. The restricted-map construction applies to general finite-dimensional supports; explicit timing results are obtained for a coherent V-type sender, a $Λ$-type receiver, and $S=\{0,2\}$, within a prescribed family of maximal exact sender reversals. With one outcome-independent full-space receiver filter, we maximize success probability at fixed total time subject to a minimum success-weighted conditional fidelity. A single preassigned recovery time yields up to three duration-dependent regimes: no sender reversal, the earliest feasible intermediate reversal, and final-time reversal. If recovery times may be randomized independently of the input and only the pooled successful ensemble must meet the fidelity requirement, the optimum over all such timing distributions mixes the two endpoints for $ΓT \ge (\ln 3)/2$. With Bell-outcome-dependent nonunitary receiver recovery, each accepted full-column-rank transfer branch can instead be reversed exactly. For the same model and sender-operation family, omitting sender reversal uniquely maximizes the exact-teleportation success probability for every finite $T>0$ at positive decay rate. Partial pathway coherence further distinguishes relative support gain from absolute recovery success: the long-time dark-state plateau survives only for fully coherent decay. These results show how receiver control capabilities and the meaning of the fidelity constraint determine whether intermediate sender recovery is useful.

Abstract: We show that criticality concentrates extensive sensing information into a local trajectory. In the rapid-readout limit of an all-to-all dissipative feedback network, a single binary trajectory has a Fisher-information rate linear in $N$ for a common weak field, as does the full record. Critical slowing does not remove this gain because the local weight of the slow mode decreases with $N$. An analytic bound guarantees that the local record retains more than half of the full information across the entire channel-parameter range. We also identify how parameter drives and observation dynamics determine local access to collective information beyond permutation symmetry. Furthermore, our sparse-readout theorem establishes a $k^{-1}$ decay of fractional information loss: only $Θ(\varepsilon^{-1})$ trajectories are needed to retain a fraction $1-\varepsilon$ of the full information rate. Any growing readout set, even of vanishing density, asymptotically recovers the full common-field Fisher-information rate.

Abstract: The Gorini-Kossakowski-Lindblad-Sudarshan (GKLS) equation, a cornerstone of open-system theory, describes Markovian semigroup dynamics in which information about the initial preparation cannot revive. Beyond this regime, information revivals are widely interpreted as backflow from a non-Markovian environment. However, a decrease followed by an increase in information retrievable from the system need not imply that information has returned from the environment [PRX Quantum 6, 020316 (2025)]. It may instead arise from noise correlations unrelated to the system's initial preparation, carried by inert degrees of freedom that never interact with the system and therefore cannot mediate backflow. Such revivals are called extrinsic; genuine backflow is reserved for revivals that cannot be explained this way. The framework developed in that work, however, assumes access to arbitrary initial system states, an assumption often incompatible with physical constraints or limited experimental control. This leaves open which properties of the available preparations are necessary for an information revival to certify genuine backflow. Here we extend the framework to allow the description of restricted families of initial states and show that genuine backflow is possible only if the family contains noncommuting states. If all available states commute, as in a classical stochastic process, every revival can be explained by a suitable inert extension and is therefore extrinsic. Noncommutativity of the available system states is thus necessary for information revivals to provide an operational signature of environmental memory.

</details>

Abstract: Quantum thermalization of closed systems -- i.e. the relaxation of local properties to their thermodynamic value along the Hamiltonian evolution -- is generically expected in non-integrable systems at finite energy density. In this work we show that quantum spin systems with at least one spin component coupled through long-range ferromagnetic interactions, and a second spin component coupled to a field, cannot in general thermalize to a paramagnetic state whenever they have a finite, negative energy per spin. The long-range interactions we consider decay as a power law of the distance, $\sim r^{-α}$ with $α< d$, where $d$ is the number of spatial dimensions of the system. The lack of thermalization is generic to an infinite family of Hamiltonians, and it is not associated to any form of integrability. It can instead be understood by the anomalous features of the thermodynamics of the system in the paramagnetic phase, which cannot be reflected in the properties of the eigenstates. This leads to a breakdown of eigenstate typicality and of the eigenstate thermalization hypothesis. Interestingly, the non-thermal states emerging at long times in the dynamics display long-range multipartite entanglement, elevating the breakdown of thermalization to a resource.

Abstract: Sample-based quantum linear solvers measure quantum Krylov states $e^{-iAT}|b\rangle$ and reconstruct the solution of $Ax=b$ classically on the observed basis states. We develop this approach for positive-definite systems that arise from elliptic partial differential equations. We show that the sampling guarantees hold in any fixed basis with an efficient transform. We then replace the variational reconstruction by a Galerkin solve on the sampled set. For this reconstruction we derive expected and high-probability energy-norm error bounds, a sample complexity in terms of the smallest sampling probability on an essential set, a computable residual certificate, and a support-completeness theorem for the dephased outcome law. We formulate the method for elliptic problems on Riemannian cross sections. In two dimensions the stiffness matrix is metric-independent in conformal coordinates, and curved vessels lead to the same matrix class. Killing symmetries of the cross section give selection rules for the measurement outcomes, and the Lie derivative of the metric gives first-order bounds for geometric perturbations. We separate operator approximations, whose effect we bound, from sampler approximations, which affect coverage but not the target. As examples we treat the implicit step of pulsatile flow in elliptic, lens-shaped, and curved ducts. In simulations of up to 12 qubits, the number of Fourier modes required for 1% error grows from 14 to 60 while the number of unknowns grows from 64 to 4096. A classical residual-greedy selection is nevertheless more efficient than quantum sampling in all cases studied, consistent with the low measured sampling efficiency. On a six-qubit trapped-ion processor, the measured outcome laws follow a replacement-noise model whose strength grows by about 0.6% per two-qubit gate, and a parity selection rule rejects shots at the predicted rate.

Abstract: Can quantum communication determine the size of an anonymous network when no size bound is known? Classical impossibility proofs rely on the covering lemma: an execution can be reproduced on a larger network with the same local structure. Quantum interference invalidates this argument - a measurement record possible on a network can become impossible on a cover. We show that the impossibility of exact counting nevertheless survives. Our main result is a quantum analogue of the covering lemma that preserves local possibilities rather than complete executions. Within a cyclic covering family, every local measurement outcome possible at a node remains possible at a corresponding node in every sufficiently large network. This holds even after information has travelled around the smaller network, beyond the reach of a simple locality argument. We extend the principle to the universal covering tree and finite covers with sufficiently long cycles. The proof combines tensor-network methods with graph coverings: a common representation across network sizes turns normalization into a constraint on which local outcomes can occur. For uniform synchronous protocols with independently prepared nodes, this support-lifting principle rules out zero-error counting and even the production of a guaranteed finite size bound on anonymous rings and the other cyclic covering families considered here. It also rules out exact leader election with termination in finite time with probability one on these families. Measurements, classical control, and local memory growing with time are allowed; no predetermined running-time bound is assumed. Quantum interference thus defeats execution lifting without enabling infallible certification of network size.

Beyond two-level and spin systems, our study puts forward the vibronic structure of molecules as a route to protected coherence subspaces, identifying a dark-state protection mechanism and a design criterion based on molecular parameters. These results lay the groundwork for our goal of using vibronic molecular systems as a resource for long-lived, controllable coherences in molecular quantum information science.

The closed-form Gaussian dynamics reveals a pitfall: extracting rates from short-time data overestimates them by more than a factor of two unless the saddle is isotropic. An isotropic cubic nonlinearity imposes the value of the ratio where the rate vanishes, so realizing two-axis twisting by pair exchange requires detuning it; a moderate mismatch already recovers almost all of the optimal depth. Abstract: Ordinal optimization selects designs that are good enough rather than optimal when evaluation is expensive and the budget is small. The alignment probability measures the chance that the selected set contains at least a prescribed number of truly good designs. We formulate quantum ordinal optimization as a class of problems that maximize this probability under a fixed oracle budget. We study exact, coherent pairwise comparisons with one selected design as a first instance, with the budget counted in comparison queries. For every classical comparison budget smaller than the number of designs, we prove that a knockout among uniformly sampled designs attains the optimum. The sample is drawn without replacement and contains one more design than the number of comparisons. For a uniformly random order, the selected design's rank has a polynomial probability law. At the same comparison budget, the maximum quantum degree is at most twice the classical maximum, a factor attained whenever there are more than twice as many designs as comparisons, so a quantum algorithm can realize rank laws that no classical algorithm with the same budget can. We determine the exact quantum optimum with one comparison for every number of designs and every good-enough set. For selecting the best of n designs, the optimal success probability with one quantum comparison is approximately 3.30/n for large n, compared with 2/n for one classical comparison. When the best design is sought, one quantum comparison gives a higher alignment probability than two classical comparisons whenever there are more than nine designs.

Abstract: Zero-added-loss multiplexing (ZALM)~[Phys. Rev. Appl. {\bf 19}, 054029 (2023)] is a midpoint-source scheme for entanglement distribution. Its quantum transmitter (QTX) employs two Sagnac sources of polarization-entangled pairs plus partial Bell-state measurements (BSMs) on their idler outputs to herald entanglement in their signal outputs, thus avoiding the switching losses suffered by other multiplexing approaches. When its Sagnac sources' spontaneous parametric downconverters (SPDCs) utilize $χ^{(2)}$ crystals with phase-matched spectral islands, ZALM avoids the tradeoff between heralding efficiency and output-state fidelity incurred when conventional-crystal SPDCs' outputs are channelized by dense wavelength-division multiplexing filters. This paper reports an end-to-end fidelity and rate analysis for islands-based ZALM that includes: SPDC multipair emissions of all orders and partial-BSM losses in the ZALM QTX; loss in propagation from the ZALM QTX to Alice and Bob's quantum receivers (QRXs); loss and mode mismatch in the mode converters employed at Alice and Bob's QRXs; and loss in the partial-BSM loading of Alice and Bob's emissive quantum memories.

Abstract: We adapt to the context of quantum theory the treatment, familiar in relativity theory, of a reference frame as a tetrad. Since a tetrad is a set of four orthonormal vectors at a spacetime point, it encodes a timelike direction, three orthogonal spatial directions, and units; and so it gives a configuration in spacetime (a spatial orientation and state of motion) of an idealized infinitesimal apparatus (``rulers and a clock"). So a natural ``first guess" is that the state of a quantum reference frame should be an assignment of complex amplitudes to a set of tetrads. We develop this idea, in terms of bundles over spacetime of bases of the tangent spaces.
  This paper is a companion to two other papers. It emphasises the main conceptual aspects of our proposal, while the other two papers give physical details. It also explains the main contrast between those two papers. Namely: (i) the first considers a single spacetime geometry, so that a quantum reference frame, on our proposal, gives a superposition of perspectives on that geometry; while (ii) the second considers the spacetime manifold with no geometry given a priori, which will imply that a quantum reference frame, on our proposal, can describe a superposition of perspectives on a superposition of geometries---not ``just" a superposition of perspectives on a single geometry. The main mathematical idea for representing this contrast will be choosing between (i) the orthonormal frame bundle, for a given spacetime geometry; (ii) the frame bundle in the usual sense of containing all bases of all spacetime points' tangent spaces.

Result: 建立了适用于有限量子态集合的确定性打包定理,提供了更精确的有限错误保证。成功应用于多个量子通信场景并恢复了已知可达速率:无辅助和纠缠辅助的经典通信、带量子边信息的分布式经典压缩、通过分束器或无源多端口器件的相干态通信。对分析中两个特定步骤给出了精确界,而非全局最优解码的声明。

Abstract: A multiple-access channel transmits messages from several senders to one receiver. For quantum outputs, the receiver must distinguish states depending on all messages, while tests for different decoding errors may not commute. In this paper, we give a self-contained account of simultaneous decoding, in which one joint measurement recovers the messages at the established achievable rates using independent codebooks. The proof combines Sen's tilted-subspace method with Cheng's analysis of the pretty-good measurement, a standard measurement for distinguishing quantum states. Auxiliary labels close to a common reference state separate the geometric directions associated with different errors. A Gram-matrix bound then allows separately chosen binary tests to certify the performance of one measurement. The labels are removed from the completed decoder, leading to a deterministic packing theorem that applies to finite state ensembles, including unequal priors. We develop the two-sender case first, with worked examples and figures, before treating any fixed number of senders. Sharper finite-error guarantees for the same decoder are developed separately, with sharp bounds for two specified steps of the analysis rather than a claim of globally optimal decoding. Applications recover known achievable rates for unassisted and entanglement-assisted classical communication, distributed classical compression with quantum side information, and coherent-state communication through a beamsplitter or passive multiport device. The common proof makes the geometric mechanism and its quantitative consequences available without repeating the construction for each application.

Abstract: We study a system of two quadratically-coupled harmonic oscillators, prepared respectively in the ground state and in a coherent state. This toy model can describe for instance the dynamics of one mode of a quantum field evolving in a harmonically-oscillating classical background. In such situations, it is well-known that, depending on the background oscillation amplitude and coupling strength, the system may exhibit parametric resonance. Using the Feynman-Vernon influence functional, we analyze the interplay between parametric resonance and quantum entanglement by perturbatively computing the purity of the mixed state resulting from tracing over one of the oscillators. We find a runaway behavior of the quantum entanglement between the two oscillators, as well as a rapid breakdown of the validity of perturbation theory, in regions of parametric instability. This provides a quantitative confirmation of various statements in the literature and is highly relevant to questions pertaining to the validity of the semiclassical approximation in coupled quantum-classical systems.

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有高通量稳定子模拟器(如Stim)仅支持Clifford门和随机泡利噪声，对于相干控制误差和热弛豫等更真实的设备噪声模型，通常使用泡利缠绕(Pauli Twirling)近似替代。这种近似方法虽然速度快，但会引入不准确性，无法真实反映实际量子设备的噪声特性，影响容错量子设计的可靠性。

Method: 提出QuasiStim系统，包含两个核心组件：(1) QuasiFold编译器前端：通过合并量子电路中等效的非泡利噪声位点，降低采样开销；(2) DeltaFrame采样后端：通过重用预计算的预测参考值对稳定子轨迹进行采样，而不是为每次shot重新模拟完整的tableau，从而显著提升采样效率。

Result: 在双变量自行车(BB)码上，QuasiFold可将所需采样次数减少超过100倍。在相干RZ噪声下的旋转表面码存储器上，当距离d从5增长到35时，DeltaFrame相比每次shot的tableau模拟器快166倍至1331倍。QuasiStim还使泡利缠绕近似(PTA)偏差可测量，结果显示该偏差不可忽略且随噪声强度、解码器和电路调度而变化。

Conclusion: QuasiStim成功实现了真实噪声下的可扩展精确量子纠错仿真，大幅提升了模拟效率和准确性。该系统揭示了泡利缠绕近似带来的非平凡偏差，为容错量子计算的设计和评估提供了更可靠的工具。代码将在发表时开源，有望成为量子纠错研究的重要工具。

Abstract: Fault-tolerant quantum design relies on simulating noisy quantum error correction (QEC) circuits with hundreds to thousands of physical qubits and large circuit depths. High-throughput stabilizer simulators, such as Stim, are the de facto tools for this regime. However, these simulators typically support only Clifford gates and stochastic Pauli noise. As a result, more realistic device noise models-such as coherent control errors and thermal relaxation-are often approximated using Pauli Twirling. This replacement offers speed but can introduce inaccuracies.
  To address this, we propose QuasiStim for scalable exact QEC simulation of realistic noise, with QuasiFold as a compiler frontend and DeltaFrame as a sampling backend. QuasiFold lowers sampling overhead by merging equivalent non-Pauli sites in the quantum circuit. DeltaFrame samples the resulting stabilizer trajectories by reusing precomputed predicted references instead of resimulating the full tableau for each shot.
  QuasiFold can reduce the number of shots by over $100\times$ on bivariate-bicycle (BB) codes. Compared to a per-shot tableau simulator, DeltaFrame is $166\times$--$1331\times$ faster on rotated surface-code memory under coherent $R_Z$ noise as $d$ grows from 5 to 35. QuasiStim also makes the PTA bias measurable: the gap is non-negligible and varies with noise strength, decoder, and circuit schedule.
  QuasiStim will be open-sourced upon publication at https://github.com/isaachyw/QuasiStim

Abstract: Entanglement witnesses are a key instrument in determining the entanglement of unknown bipartite qubit systems without requiring a full tomography. Riccardi et al. Res. 2, 033421 (2020)], in its original formulation, employs the orbital gradient and Hessian in a Newton--Laphson procedure. Rev. Res. 2, 043158 (2020)] to orbital rotation parameters. Based on the frequency structure implied by the algebraic properties of the orbital rotation generators, our method determines each orbital rotation angle from a nine-point energy profile. The energy landscape is evaluated by classically transforming the Hamiltonian integrals and contracting them with the reduced density matrices already measured for the VQE energy evaluation. This allows for orbital optimization without evaluating the orbital gradient or Hessian. By building on the core benefits of the OO-UCC framework, our method provides a gradient-free and hyperparameter-free orbital-optimization procedure. State-vector simulations for LiH in the STO-3G basis show that SOO-UCCD reproduces UCCSD-level accuracy along the bond-stretching curve and exhibits macro-iteration convergence comparable to that of L-BFGS-based orbital optimization.

Abstract: Quantum mechanics and general relativity are remarkably successful, yet their joint predictions remain beyond direct experimental reach. Photonic interferometers operating in a gravitational field offer a promising route to test this regime: gravitational redshift differentiates photon propagation between vertically separated arms, leading to a predicted phase shift and reduction in interference visibility. These effects are minute on laboratory scales, motivating existing proposals to consider large interferometer areas to accumulate them geometrically. Indeed, such schemes typically require kilometre-scale optical paths to produce appreciable interferometric signals. Here, we propose a strategy that replaces geometric accumulation with spectral enhancement by coupling the two interferometer arms to optical scatterers based on ultranarrow atomic transitions. These transitions serve as local spectral references: gravitational redshift changes the photon's detuning from each resonance, producing large differences in reflection amplitude and hence substantial visibility loss and interferometric phase shift already for centimetre-scale height differences. We quantify the experimental requirements and assess the feasibility of implementation with current or near-term technology. Our proposal thus opens a route to tabletop optical tests of the interplay between quantum mechanics and general relativity.

Abstract: Topological insulators lie outside the standard Landau paradigm: Instead of being characterized by local order parameters and spontaneous symmetry breaking, their defining properties are encoded in the global structure of their quantum states and manifested as robust boundary states. Although these boundary modes can be detected spectroscopically through the local density of states, determining the underlying bulk topology, for example through nested Wilson loops, remains an outstanding experimental challenge. Here we use up to 728 qubits in a superconducting analogue-digital quantum simulator to realize and probe a hierarchy of one-, two-, and three-dimensional topological insulators governed by effective tight-binding Hamiltonians. By reconstructing complex propagators from real-space measurements, we obtain complementary boundary and bulk characterizations: local densities of states reveal localized boundary modes, whereas experimentally reconstructed band projectors yield bulk topological invariants, culminating in a three-dimensional octupole moment extracted through third nested Wilson loops. These results establish a route to realizing, characterizing, and manipulating topologically protected states in programmable three-dimensional quantum matter.

Abstract: Small particles respond to light through electric and magnetic patterns called multipoles. Their interference determines the direction of scattered light and affects the decay of nearby emitters. We show how to represent these responses with quantum bits (qubits) and use quantum circuits to calculate scattering and the Purcell effect, the change in an emitter's decay rate caused by its surroundings. The circuits can encode known multipoles or estimate unknown ones from Maxwell's equations at the particle surface. We test the method against classical theory using an ideal quantum simulator and real IBM and IonQ quantum computers. For a silicon nanoparticle of radius 78 nm illuminated at 679 nm, IonQ gives a forward directivity of 2.984, close to the ideal dipolar value 3, quantifying the concentration of scattered light in the forward direction. For an emitter 10 nm from a gold nanoparticle and emitting at 510 nm, IBM measurements yield a calculated total decay rate 20.48 times its vacuum value versus 20.47 from theory, with correction for errors in reading the qubit states. We also establish how the number of multipoles and the required accuracy set the demands on qubits and measurements. These results motivate quantum-assisted design studies of directional radiation and emitter decay in nanophotonic systems.

Abstract: We show that a common quantum-jump feedback protocol can permanently reverse the nonequilibrium free-energy ordering of two prescribed initial states while preserving their Gibbs equilibrium. The construction exploits collective compensation between monitored channels: their feedback corrections cancel at equilibrium but remain active during relaxation. This balance preserves free-energy monotonicity while allowing the relaxation modes to be reshaped. We prove that eliminating the complete slow-sector contribution of the initially higher-free-energy state, while retaining it for the comparison state, produces distinct asymptotic decay exponents. Their free-energy ratio then vanishes at long times, guaranteeing a permanent thermodynamic quantum Mpemba inversion under the same controlled dynamics. A thermal qutrit realizes the mechanism through coherence interference and exhibits distinct detection thresholds for spectral selection and exact slow-sector cancellation under feedback retuning.

Abstract: Precise manipulation of qubits (spins) requires strong suppression of noise arising from couplings between qubits and their environment, as well as from driving field fluctuations. We propose a double-pulse sequence and an eighteen-pulse sequence to realize a single spin pi-rotation under off-resonance conditions (instead of conventional on-resonance or near-resonance conditions). It is proved that the latter sequence performs much better than the former in simultaneously suppressing both dephasing and driving field noise. As a proof of principle, we experimentally demonstrate, by implementing both sequences in an NMR spin system with a polarized initial spin state, that the off-resonance pi-rotation fidelity reaches as high as 0.9992 +/- 0.0008 for the double-pulse sequence and 0.9997 +/- 0.0006 for the eighteen-pulse sequence. In addition, the noise-tolerance range of the eighteen-pulse sequence is about 20 percent of the driving field strength, approximately three times wider than that of the double-pulse sequence. Besides its potential application in the NMR community, our proposal provides an alternative practical way to accurately control qubits by suppressing the coexisting transversal and longitudinal noises.

Method: QIAP采用受直角基和对角基选择启发的双分支验证结构：(1)直角分支中，证明者打开基于哈希的承诺，验证者检查其与注册轮次令牌的一致性；(2)对角分支中，证明者返回绑定验证者随机数、轮次标识符和提交承诺的HMAC标签，而不暴露轮次特定子密钥。框架基于哈希、HMAC、新鲜性、安全注册、受保护存储和高熵密钥等安全假设。认证成功后，BB84模块估算量子误码率(QBER)，当测量值达到或超过配置阈值时中止会话。

Abstract: Authentication in quantum-aware communication settings requires mechanisms that verify user legitimacy while also assessing whether the communication channel exhibits excessive disturbance. This paper presents QIAP, a stateful hybrid framework that combines classical commitment-based challenge-response authentication with BB84-based quantum-channel monitoring. QIAP uses a dual-branch verification structure inspired by rectilinear and diagonal basis selection. In the rectilinear branch, the prover opens a hash-based commitment and the verifier checks consistency with the registered round token. In the diagonal branch, the prover returns an HMAC tag bound to the verifier nonce, round identifier, and submitted commitment without disclosing the round-specific sub-secret. Under the stated hash, HMAC, freshness, secure-registration, protected-storage, and high-entropy-secret assumptions, QIAP provides computational resistance to the modeled wrong-secret and replay attempts and protects the long-term master secret from direct recovery. After successful authentication, the BB84 module estimates the quantum bit error rate (QBER) and aborts the session when the measured value reaches or exceeds the configured threshold. In the reported simulation, no successful authentication was observed among 1,000 tested wrong-secret attempts per branch. The baseline and full intercept-resend conditions produced mean QBER values of approximately 0.02 and 0.25, respectively. The classical authentication stage required approximately 0.6 ms, and the implemented authentication messages required approximately 113 bytes per round. These results indicate that QIAP can provide a lightweight hybrid framework for combining secret-based identity verification with QBER-based channel-disturbance assessment under the stated threat model.

</details>

Abstract: We consider an orthogonal basis of $\mathbb C^2\otimes\mathbb C^2$ with three entangled states and one product state. We then show that a collective separable measurement on the two copies does. Whether two copies suffice for LOCC remains open.

Abstract: Quantum teleportation, a key building block for quantum technologies, enables distant parties, Alice and Bob, to transfer an unknown quantum state using entanglement, local operations, and classical communication. In this work, we propose a quantum teleportation protocol for single-photon time-frequency continuous variables, where time is the time of arrival at a detector. We detail the operational requirements of the protocol, notably the nonlinear interactions needed for the photon-photon time-frequency entangling gate. By explicitly treating the detector position as the reference frame for time of arrival, we show that, even with ideal classical and quantum channels, the input-output fidelity is not unity unless the receiving party can correct the detector-position mismatch. This results in a trade-off between teleportation fidelity and the temporal bandwidth of the input photon wavepacket. The protocol itself can be used for remote detector synchronization. In particular, we show that time-frequency cat and Gottesman-Kitaev-Preskill states provide an internal temporal ruler through fidelity revivals. We further derive the classical and best-copy fidelity thresholds for single-photon Gaussian wavepackets, finding that the relevant vacuum-equivalent shot noise is input-dependent, in contrast with quadrature-based continuous-variable quantum teleportation. Our proposal derives from a quantum theory of space-time events, whereafter we establish space as a reference for the time degree of freedom, thereby grounding our work in ongoing research on reference frames and superselection rules in quantum optics and quantum information. Beyond protocols harnessing time-frequency continuous variables of single photons, this work points toward remote synchronization in quantum communication architectures.

We demonstrate deterministic convergence over a broad range of parameters and show that the performance degradation near the stability boundary is governed by the shrinking Bogoliubov quasiparticle gap.   <summary>Details</summary>

Result: 获得三个主要结果:(1)对于k≥4的情况,证明了Ω(k log² n)的下界,改进了之前最好的经典和量子下界;(2)对于任意固定常数α>0,存在常数δ(α)>0使得当k>n^α时,Q_ε(Tarski(n,k))=Ω(kn^(δ(α))),特别地,当k>n^(2/203)时获得Ω(kn^0.0072)下界,这是首个对维度具有线性依赖且对宽度具有多项式依赖的下界;(3)对于指数宽度网格(n=2^Θ(k)),证明了(k/log k)^Ω(log k)的超多项式下界。

Conclusion: 本文通过硬度自举技术显著改进了Tarski不动点问题的量子查询下界,填补了理论上的重要空白。特别是超多项式下界结果具有重要的计算复杂性意义,排除了通过通用多项式查询黑盒Tarski求解器在多项式时间内解决简单随机博弈和ARRIVAL问题的可能路径,表明这些问题可能具有本质困难性。这些结果不仅推进了对Tarski不动点问题复杂度的理解,也为相关计算问题提供了新的复杂性理论洞察。

Abstract: The Tarski fixed-point problem asks for a fixed point of a monotone map on a $k$-dimensional grid lattice of width $n$ and has applications in game theory, economics, and verification. Substantial gaps remain between the known upper and lower bounds on its quantum query complexity.
  We prove two hardness bootstrapping theorems for quantum query lower bounds. Starting from a hard instance family whose instances have pair-wise incomparable unique fixed points, and a lower bound established by the nonnegative adversary method, we bootstrap this lower bound by (i) a factor of $Ω(t)$ when the dimension is multiplied by $t$ and the width remains unchanged, and (ii) a factor of $Ω(\log m)$ when the width is multiplied by $m$ and the dimension is doubled. Applying these theorems, we obtain three consequences: (i) an $Ω(k\log^2 n)$ lower bound for $k\geq 4$, improving previously best-known classical and quantum lower bounds; (ii) for every fixed constant $α>0$, there exists a constant $δ(α)>0$ such that $Q_ε(\text{Tarski}(n,k))=Ω(kn^{δ(α)})$ whenever $k>n^α$, and in particular, an $Ω(kn^{0.0072})$ lower bound for $k>n^{2/203}$, the first lower bound with simultaneous linear dependence on the dimension and polynomial dependence on the width; and (iii) a superpolynomial lower bound of $(k/\log k)^{Ω(\log k)}$ for exponentially wide grids, where $n=2^{Θ(k)}$. Abstract: Genuine multipartite entanglement (GME) is a valuable resource for quantum technologies, but quantifying it in many-body systems can be demanding since measures defined through optimizations over bipartitions are often assumed to require large joint measurements and many measurement settings. We introduce the concepts of GME evaluation length and GME evaluation number, which capture, respectively, the largest subsystem that must be jointly measured and the number of distinct subsystems that need to be measured to evaluate multipartite entanglement measures defined via bipartition optimizations. Taking the genuine multipartite concurrence (GMC) as a canonical example, we show that, for $N$-qubit Haar-random pure states, the GMC can be evaluated using only $N$ single-qubit purity measurements. Thus, for typical many-body states, evaluating the GMC requires considering only the $N$ single-site bipartitions, rather than the exponentially many distinct bipartitions. We further corroborate this behavior numerically for physically accessible states generated by local random quantum circuits. Our results show that the practical cost of quantifying GME can be dramatically lower than suggested by its formal definition, establishing measurement complexity as a complementary operational lens for multipartite entanglement.

Abstract: In this PhD thesis, we take an axiomatic approach to studying multivariate divergences, which are measures of the information content of a tuple of classical or quantum states. These divergences are studied in the context of large-sample and catalytic convertibility of tuples of states. This type of convertibility is characterized by the ordering of an axiomatically defined family of divergences. In this thesis, we identify this family of divergences explicitly for two scenarios, one classical and one quantum. In the classical setting, we show that the relevant divergences are certain generalizations of the bivariate Rényi divergences. Unlike previous studies, we do not require the distributions to have the same support, yielding interesting new divergences. In the quantum setting, we consider pairs of classical-quantum states with pure component states, and find that majorization is characterized by all $α$-$z$ relative entropies for $α<1$. Abstract: Quantum random number generators are crucial for information security, yet existing implementations are hindered by bulky optics, device imperfections, and state alignment constraints. Here, using a compact metasurface platform, we achieve polarization-independent certified randomness by establishing a quantitative quantum graph framework. Abstract: An isolated quantum system evolves reversibly, yet a small subsystem can lose nearly all information about its initial state. Conserved quantities may retain some of this information, depending on how strongly they overlap with local observables. Here we consider a subsystem in an arbitrary initial state coupled to a maximally mixed bath. We show that the worst-input mean-square equilibration error, maximised over window origins, is bounded from above and below by the mean square of a complete local autocorrelation, with the stationary part subtracted. A correlation record over a finite interval therefore controls the equilibration error in every interval of the same length. The result allows arbitrary degeneracies of energies and gaps. We apply it to two static Hamiltonian families. For fixed spin subsystems in the one-particle sector of a bounded-range periodic clock, fixed-accuracy thermalisation is guaranteed on windows that grow only slightly faster than linearly with system size, despite exponentially small positive energies. A random commuting model with logarithmic interaction range retains an extensive set of conserved observables. Its expected mean-square error is bounded by a finite-size term and an inverse-time term. The inverse-time dependence is unavoidable because of the initial transient. These examples show how local relaxation can coexist with exact conservation laws and a finely spaced spectrum.

Abstract: We propose Pathfinder-BP, a recursive decoder for topological quantum codes that combines belief propagation with controlled bit-flipping. Upon convergence failure, the decoder recursively explores alternative error patterns by tentatively flipping low-confidence variables. On surface codes, Pathfinder-BP achieves a noise threshold and logical error rate comparable to minimum-weight perfect matching (MWPM). Motivation: 随机化量子算法通过牺牲线路深度换取更多采样数来估计分子观测量期望值，被认为是实现早期容错量子优势的有前景路径。然而，对于工业相关的分子系统，由此产生的测量开销是否仍然可行尚不清楚。本研究旨在通过实际系统的基准测试来评估该算法在工业应用中的可行性，特别是针对制药行业相关的细胞色素P450系统。

Abstract: Randomized quantum algorithms for the estimation of expectation values of molecular observables offer a promising route to early fault-tolerant quantum advantage by trading circuit depth for an increased number of samples. Our numerical simulations reveal that the analytical error bounds significantly overestimate the actual sampling error, whereas the analytical bound on the required imaginary evolution time is comparatively tight, exceeding a direct DMRG estimate by roughly 44% for the active space where the comparison is feasible. The measurement overhead nevertheless remains prohibitively high: even for the smallest active spaces, the expectation-value estimation alone requires around 1010 samples, scaling to beyond 1015 for the largest active spaces considered. These results suggest that substantial improvements in measurement strategies or algorithmic scaling are necessary before randomized early-fault-tolerant algorithms become competitive for industrial quantum chemistry applications.

Conclusion: magic-free certification协议为通用容错量子电路提供了一种高效、可扩展的认证方案,不消耗宝贵的magic资源,样本复杂度具有良好的可扩展性。该方法通过巧妙利用陷阱电路与目标电路在噪声下的不可区分性,实现了对逻辑错误率的有效验证,为实际量子设备上容错算法的可靠执行提供了重要的质量保证工具,对推动容错量子计算的实用化具有重要意义。

Abstract: As quantum hardware enters the fault-tolerant regime, algorithms are beginning to be executed on encoded qubits, yet scalable certification of how well they run remains an open problem. Here we introduce magic-free certification, a certification protocol for universal fault-tolerant circuits that consumes no magic, the resource that generally places them beyond efficient classical simulation and is by far the most expensive element of a fault-tolerant computation. The protocol is code- and decoder-agnostic, runs interleaved with the target circuit on the same device, and has a sample complexity that grows linearly with the number of logical qubits and non-Clifford gates while remaining decoupled from the logical error rate itself. Given a predefined error tolerance $p_L^*$, the protocol determines at a chosen confidence level whether the device executes the target circuit at a logical error rate below $p_L^*$. The method relies on trap circuits, copies of the target with every magic state replaced by a stabilizer state, whose outcomes are deterministic and which, being indistinguishable from the target to stochastic Pauli noise, fail at the same rate. We illustrate the protocol in Steane-code simulations of a magic state injection gadget and a quantum Fourier transform.

</details>

Conclusion: 本研究揭示了量子态纹理在霍金辐射下的退化机制和重新分配规律,发现了"正交性保护"这一新的量子资源保护机制。研究结果为理解黑洞背景下量子资源的行为提供了新视角,表明在特定条件下量子资源可以抵抗引力效应的破坏,并且可以在不同自由度之间进行重新分配。这些发现对量子信息在极端引力环境下的处理和保护具有重要理论意义。

Abstract: Quantum state texture (QST) is a recently proposed quantum resource that characterizes the nonuniformity of a density matrix in a given computational basis. In the resource theory of QST, the Fourier states f_k are maximally resourceful states, attaining the maximum QST measure. We study the QST behavior of three maximally resourceful states in a two-mode Dirac field under Hawking radiation from a Schwarzschild black hole, using a fidelity-based measure. We consider three cases: only mode B undergoes radiation, only mode A undergoes radiation, and both modes undergo radiation simultaneously. Our results show that for single-mode radiation, if the mode unaffected by radiation is orthogonal to the single-mode textureless state, the QST of the overall output state remains maximal and is independent of the Hawking temperature; we call this phenomenon orthogonality protection. When both modes undergo radiation simultaneously, orthogonality protection vanishes, and the QST of all three Fourier states decreases with increasing Hawking temperature. Notably, the QST measure of f_3 under two-mode radiation is higher than that under only mode B radiation, indicating that QST can be redistributed among modes: QST gained by one mode under radiation can partially compensate for QST lost by the other. We further provide a general resource-theoretic explanation of orthogonality protection, showing that this phenomenon is independent of the specific form of the QST measure. These results reveal how QST degrades under Hawking radiation and provide a new perspective on the redistribution of quantum resources in black hole backgrounds.

Conclusion: 通过引入基于关系性记录的新本体论框架，贝尔不可能定理被转化为可能定理。局域因果性与量子统计不再矛盾，量子预测的实在论解释可以是非阴谋性的，所有因果影响在操作层面都有体现。这为量子力学提供了一种新的实在论诠释途径。

Abstract: Bell's theorem is popularly stated as the inconsistency of quantum theory with local realism. Realism is here formalized by the ontological models framework, where elements of reality are points of a measurable space and probabilities obey the Kolmogorov axioms. In the Bell scenario, locally causal ontological models posit a common cause supporting a global valuation, a joint assignment of outcomes to all measurements at once, across incompatible contexts. Within this notion of realism, Bell's theorem makes any realist explanation of the quantum predictions conspiratorial: any mechanism, whether superluminal, superdeterministic, or retrocausal, must be finely tuned so as never to manifest operationally. We redefine realism through a new ontological framework that generalizes the standard one. The elements of reality, which we call records, are no longer points but relational parts of a point-free generalization of a topological space (sublocales of a locale). A record shared by two incompatible contexts establishes the sameness of a measurement across them, without such pairwise identities composing into a global valuation, and the common cause is then a web of such records. Within this framework, Bell's no-go theorem becomes a go theorem: local causality, restated for records, is no longer at odds with the quantum statistics. This is possible because modularity, the inclusion-exclusion law of classical probability, fails across incompatible contexts, so that the probability theory generalizes Kolmogorov's. We exhibit explicit models reproducing several quantum strategies, including the optimal CHSH violation. More broadly, locally causal models of this kind attain every Bell-CHSH value up to the algebraic maximum. A realist explanation of the quantum predictions need not be conspiratorial; every causal influence posited by these models is reflected in the operational predictions.

</details>
